Rev799 note: internal clipboard rows now carry runtime authority, and destructive cut actions preflight clipboard-write authority before deleting text. `CutLine` accumulation now appends through the guarded clipboard helper instead of mutating `clipboard_items` directly. See `docs/757-clipboard-register-authority.md`.

Latest tiny landing (rev799): the destructive/register audit covers the internal clipboard and the edit-before-deny cut/cutline boundary; scripts cannot use Cut or accumulated CutLine to erase text or append into a trusted clipboard without explicit authority.

## Rev799 clipboard register + cut preflight boundary

- Internal clipboard state now carries runtime provenance (`clipboard_authority`) like recent rows, prompt history, marks, selections, help/message history, and undo/redo.
- Script-origin clipboard reads/writes are same-origin by default; trusted/cross-origin reads require `cap.clipboard-read`, and trusted/cross-origin writes require `cap.clipboard-write`.
- Paste uses the guarded clipboard snapshot, and failed plugin callback rollback restores clipboard value, kind, serial, source bit, and authority.
- `Cut` and `CutLine` now check clipboard-write authority before deleting text. `CutLine` accumulation uses `append_clipboard_items()` rather than direct list mutation.

Rev799 note: internal clipboard rows now carry runtime authority. `docs/757-clipboard-register-authority.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev799): lower-authority scripts cannot read trusted/user clipboard text through hostcalls or Paste, cannot overwrite trusted clipboard rows without `cap.clipboard-write`, and failed plugin callbacks no longer leave half-failed clipboard state behind.

Rev798 note: undo/redo entries carry runtime authority, and persisted recent/prompt-history rows restore as lower-authority persistence rows instead of trusted editor state. `docs/755-undo-redo-authority-boundary.md` and `docs/756-persisted-history-provenance.md` record the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev798): the recovery-register lane now covers edit replay and persistence restore. Scripts can use their own undo/history rows, but trusted edits and disk-loaded history are no longer borrowed implicitly.

Rev797 note: `cap.history-clear` is now explicitly cleanup/prune authority, not help-history replay authority. `helpback` and `helpforward` through trusted docs-help rows still require row ownership and fail before any buffer switch. `docs/754-helphistory-replay-cap-boundary.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev797): help-history replay now follows the same delayed-authority rule as jumplist navigation: cleanup caps do not borrow trusted navigation state.

- Narrowed the `cap.history-clear` bypass so it covers destructive cleanup/prune operations but not help-history replay.
- Kept same-origin script helpback working through row authority.
- Added a regression proving trusted `cap.history-clear` does not let scripts replay trusted helpback rows.
- Next: audit persisted history restore provenance for recent/help/message history.

Rev796 note: `cap.history-clear` is now a first-class unsafe capability (`ed.history-clear`) for trusted automation that is intentionally allowed to clear broader editor history/evidence registers. `docs/753-history-clear-capability-wiring.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev796): the destructive-register authority lane now has a coherent capability story. Message, help, recent, and jumplist state stay protected by default; trusted users can explicitly opt scripts into broader history clears.

Rev794 note: recent-file MRU rows now carry runtime authority. Script-origin clears can no longer erase trusted/user or other-origin recent entries, incidental script open/save MRU updates skip instead of evicting protected rows, and rollback snapshots preserve recent authority. `docs/751-recent-register-authority.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev793): the jumplist is now treated like a recovery register instead of throwaway cursor trivia. ## Rev792 selection recovery stack authority

- Landed `EditorBuffer.sel_stack_authority` so saved-selection snapshots carry the same runtime provenance discipline as commands, keybindings, hooks, timers, macros, and named marks.
- Lower-authority scripts can no longer pop or clear trusted/user saved selections or another script's saved-selection snapshots.
- `ed.set-selections` now preflights the implicit saved-selection clear before mutating cursor state or consuming the caller operand.
- Transaction snapshots preserve the authority sidecar so rollback does not launder selection snapshots back to trusted/default provenance.
- Next adjacent audit targets: jump history, recent clear, docs help-history mutation, and message-log destructive hostcalls.

## Rev790 mark authority and plugin rollback coverage

Rev790 note: named marks now carry runtime authority sidecar metadata. Scripts can create and retarget their own marks, but cannot retarget trusted/user marks or marks owned by another script origin. Plugin-created marks carry plugin group/root/generation provenance and now participate in plugin callback/load/reload rollback, group retag, and unload cleanup.

Concrete forward movement: `ed.mark-set` now preserves the requested mark name when a policy denial fires, `mark NAME` reports policy denials cleanly, mark authority stays coherent through save-as/macro/with-undo snapshots, and failed plugin callback-style mutations no longer strand transient marks.

Docs: `docs/747-mark-authority-plugin-rollback.md`.

## Rev789 mutable value rollback snapshots

Rev789 note: rollback boundaries now snapshot portable mutable Micromax values, not just container membership. Core `catch`, scoped editor hostcalls, plugin execution/callback rollback, and script-context `Editor.input` restoration use `src/micromax/value_snapshot.py` so failed quotations and lower-authority callbacks cannot mutate caller-owned `list`/`dict` values in place and leave the mutation behind.

Concrete forward movement: the rev0788 stack-rollback promise now covers mutable list/map operands; script callbacks cannot poison nested action-input scratch values; plugin callback rollback uses the same stack-like value discipline; and core `catch` has a sharper portable-container contract for future hosts.

Docs: `docs/746-value-snapshot-mutable-rollback.md`.

## Rev788 bridge stack snapshots and mark-only transactions

Rev788 note: editor bridge hostcalls are pop-free at the host boundary, scoped helper failures restore a full VM stack snapshot after consuming their own operands, and mark-only `ed.with-undo` groups now create undo/redo entries.

Concrete forward movement: a failing helper quotation can no longer eat caller operands with `drop` before raising; the bridge has a static guard against raw consuming VM pop helpers; and named mark creation/retargeting participates in `ed.with-undo` even when no text changes.

Docs: `docs/745-bridge-popfree-markonly-transaction.md`.

## Rev787 runtime transaction and scope-control hostcall boundaries

Rev787 note: runtime transaction boundaries now include global marks, cursor-id allocation evidence, all-open-buffer cursorstate, message-log scopes, and timer/control stack evidence. The focused precursor notes are `docs/742-withundo-marks-scopepreflight.md` and `docs/743-cursorstate-message-timer-boundary.md`; `docs/744-runtime-transaction-scope-boundaries.md` is the combined handoff.

Concrete forward movement: `ed.with-undo` now rolls back/undoes named mark changes with the grouped text changes, `ed.with-cursorstate` restores active buffer identity and all captured cursor/selection state, malformed transaction/timer calls keep their operands, and script-denied trusted timer cancellation leaves the id visible and the timer scheduled.

Docs: `docs/744-runtime-transaction-scope-boundaries.md`.

## Rev783 direct fsync and selection/cursor argument boundary

## rev0784 — with-undo transaction and runtime hostcall stack evidence

- Fixed `ed.with-undo` so failing cross-buffer quotations roll back every open buffer, not just the active buffer.
- Added one-step undo/redo for successful cross-buffer `ed.with-undo` groups.
- Migrated high-risk runtime mutation hostcalls toward peek-then-commit stack boundaries.
- Added `MXDOCTOR_PREFLIGHT_GROUP_SIZE` for bounded doctor preflight grouping.


Rev783 note: direct non-atomic saves now honor `save.fsync=true`, and cursor/selection/range hostcalls now preflight typed/list arguments before consuming VM-stack evidence or mutating live state. `docs/740-direct-fsync-selection-boundary.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev783): `save.atomic=false` no longer drops explicit fsync requests; malformed cursor/line/range/selection/cursorstate hostcalls preserve their original arguments; and bad selection/cursorstate setters validate fully before touching live cursor state or allocating cursor IDs.

This is a runtime-facing repair on top of the direct-save and delayed-authority work already present in the cloudtainer. The direct writer now honors explicit fsync, and the remaining higher-risk cursor/selection hostcalls fail before erasing debug evidence or partially mutating editor state.

Docs: `docs/740-direct-fsync-selection-boundary.md`.

Rev782 note: active keymap modes now carry script/plugin provenance, so a script can no longer push a trusted mode and let a later keypress execute that mode's trusted binding as ambient user authority. Scripts also cannot pop/clear trusted active keymodes or shadow trusted capture modes. `mxdoctor` also avoids repeated child-process churn in the bounded risk lane after this turn exposed stale-workdir/file-scan waste in the cloudtainer. `docs/739-active-keymode-authority.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev782): active keymode state joins the delayed-authority model: script-activated one-shot and persistent modes keep script authority during later dispatch, while trusted capture/keymode state is protected from lower-authority pop/clear/shadow mutations.

Rev778 note: this landing combines two trust-boundary audits. Host-facing capability failures now preserve operation evidence and external URL dispatch is narrowed to the documented `http`/`https`/`mailto` scheme set. Runtime registrations now have a script/plugin provenance guard: lower-authority script code cannot overwrite trusted commands, keybindings, hooks, or timers, plugin reload staging has a narrow previous-generation replacement witness, scripted `cd` requires `cap.fs-chdir` and respects `cap.fs-root`, and scripted dirty-buffer force discard requires `cap.buffer-discard`.

Concrete forward movement: denied `ed.open-url`, `ed.shell`, `ed.fs-read`, `ed.fs-list`, `ed.fs-stat`, `ed.open`, and `ed.require` calls leave operation evidence on the VM stack; unsupported URL schemes never reach the opener; runtime mutation now goes through checked helpers for commands, keybindings, timers, and hooks; plugin `deinit` can still clean up its own registrations; transactional reload can stage same-plugin replacements without letting stale callbacks borrow authority; `close!`/`closeall!`/`only!`/`quit!`/`revert!` are capability-gated when they would discard dirty buffers from script context; and scripted `cd` is gated plus root-contained.

Remaining risk: this is still application-level provenance inside one process, not an OS sandbox. Future registries and bridge hostcalls need to use the same checked-helper/preflight pattern rather than adding new raw mutation or host-dispatch surfaces.

Docs: `docs/732-hostcall-url-boundary.md`, `docs/733-runtime-registration-chdir-bufferdiscard.md`, `docs/31-host-api.md`, `docs/32-capabilities.md`.

## Rev777 readonly hostcall boundary and plugin callback generations

Rev777 note: direct Micromax text-edit hostcalls now share the effective-readonly/protected-buffer boundary, and deferred plugin callbacks now carry a loaded-plugin generation token in addition to script origin and package root. Rev777 adds `src/micromax_editor/edit_boundary.py`, routes `ed.set-text`, `ed.replace-range`, `ed.delete-range`, and `ed.replace-selections` through the readonly guard before consuming operation arguments, and tightens plugin callback provenance so callbacks from an older reload generation cannot borrow a later plugin's wordlist/root at the same path.

This is intentionally runtime-facing: the recent trust-boundary work had made script/plugin authority much cleaner, but raw text-edit hostcalls and root-only plugin callback provenance were both places where an older/lower-authority path could still surprise a long-lived editor.

Docs: `docs/730-readonly-hostcall-edit-boundary.md`, `docs/731-plugin-callback-generation-token.md`, `docs/134-readonly.md`, `docs/31-host-api.md`.

## Rev776 deferred callback rollback, plugin provenance, and script option policy

Rev776 note: deferred plugin callbacks now have a stronger failure/provenance boundary, and script-originated option mutation now protects host-adjacent non-capability knobs. Failed plugin keybindings, macro steps, hooks/timers, and `ed.cmd-add` command callbacks roll back partial helper definitions and runtime registrations; plugin authority is anchored to a currently loaded plugin root rather than a spoofable `plugin:*` group string; and scripts can no longer seed external clipboard commands, redirect persistence, disable URL confirmation, or relax save/readonly/autosave policy for later user actions.

This was intentionally runtime-facing rather than registry work: the patch closes concrete delayed-authority and partial-load failure modes in plugin callbacks, prevents lower-authority scripts from poisoning host behavior after the script has returned, and blocks editor-owned autosave from becoming a delayed disk-write path for script-originated edits.

Docs: `docs/726-plugin-callback-failure-rollback.md`, `docs/727-plugin-group-provenance-guard.md`, `docs/728-script-option-policy-hostknobs.md`, `docs/729-script-dirty-autosave-taint.md`.

## Rev775 plugin callback wordlist context

Rev775 note: plugin-created deferred callbacks now restore plugin dictionary context, not just script authority and package-local load roots. Keybindings, timers, hooks, macros, and ed.cmd-add command callbacks enter the loaded plugin wordlist as CURRENT with that wordlist first in search order, so callback-time include/require definitions stay private instead of leaking into the global/Forth wordlist. Stale roots from unloaded plugins are no longer honored when a plugin manager is present. docs/725-plugin-callback-wordlist-context.md records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev775): plugin callback-time includes now define helper words inside the loaded plugin wordlist, plugin keybindings can call plugin-local words without spelling use NAME, and unloaded-plugin callbacks no longer retain stale package-root load authority.

This is intentionally runtime-facing rather than registry churn: the package-local root and script-origin bit were already present, but the missing wordlist/current-context restoration meant delayed plugin helpers could become global definitions. The new context makes deferred plugin callbacks look like plugin execution again at the point where code actually runs.

Docs: `docs/725-plugin-callback-wordlist-context.md`.

## Rev774 deferred callback authority and plugin-local roots

Rev774 note: deferred callback authority now covers keybindings, macros, timers, and hooks with plugin-local source roots. Script/plugin-created keybindings and macro steps carry script_context plus an optional plugin_load_root, key dispatch/timer pump/hook execution/macro replay restore those labels, and binding description updates no longer erase lower-authority provenance. docs/724-deferred-keybinding-macro-authority.md records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev774): script/plugin-created deferred callbacks can no longer wait until after the script exits and then mutate cap.* as ambient editor authority; plugin callbacks also keep package-local include roots without granting global cap.fs-require.

This turn also corrected a half-landed keybinding provenance seam: `ed.bind` could pass `plugin_load_root`, but `Keymap.bind(...)` did not yet accept or preserve it. The fix is intentionally small and runtime-facing: deferred callbacks carry origin labels, and execution restores those labels at the point where authority matters.

Docs: `docs/724-deferred-keybinding-macro-authority.md`.

Rev773 note: this closes the nested/deferred script-code boundary that rev772 left exposed. The editor VM now installs a script-context load policy for core include/require/reload/unrequire, ed.require evaluates loaded files inside script_context, ed.run action chains and direct ed.macro-play replays run with script authority, plugin reload command/hostcall paths require cap.fs-require from script context, and script-originated timers/hooks keep script authority when they fire later. docs/723-script-code-load-deferred-boundary.md records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev773): script code-loading, action chains, direct macro replay, plugin reloads, and delayed timer/hook callbacks no longer become more privileged than the script that initiated them; trusted init/direct interactive VM evaluation remains the explicit way to grant capabilities.

Rev771 note: this continues the file/script trust lane with concrete containment and data-loss fixes rather than registry churn. New fd-backed helpers in `src/micromax_editor/file_access.py` now bind script-visible read/list/stat/recovery/open preflights to the file or directory that was actually opened, so a late symlink swap after cap-root preflight is refused instead of leaking outside the authorized tree. Save-as/write paths now treat a previously missing target as a create-only commit: a file created after the final freshness check trips `FileFreshnessConflict` instead of being overwritten by `os.replace`. Scripted saves with `mkparents` use `file_write.ensure_parent_directory(...)`; under `cap.fs-root`, capable POSIX hosts walk from an opened root directory fd and avoid following parent symlinks. `ed.require` now has its own `cap.fs-require` code-load authority, and `mxtest --max-new-chunks` can write bounded partial aggregate evidence without pretending the rest ran. `docs/721-mkparents-require-capboundary.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev771): fd-contained file reads/list/stat close the remaining script hostcall symlink-swap gap, missing-target saves no longer overwrite a file born between freshness check and commit, `mkparents` is contained, and `ed.require` is no longer enabled by plain text-read authority.

## Rev770 dir-fd writer, filesystem hostcalls, mxtest signal cleanup, and plugin dictionary rollback

This turn stayed on risky state/file/validation boundaries rather than expanding registry trail. The save writer now uses an opened parent directory fd on capable POSIX hosts so temp creation, freshness checks, cleanup, and atomic replace are tied to the directory that was actually authorized. A parent-directory swap after temp creation now fails closed and cleans the live temp from the original directory.

Script-visible filesystem hostcalls were also brought into the final-containment lane. `src/micromax_editor/fs_hostcalls.py` owns `ed.fs-read`, `ed.fs-list`, and `ed.fs-stat`; scripted `open`, `ed.open`, and cap-rooted palette opens now pass a containment root into `Editor.open_file(...)`. `ed.fs-list` uses `lstat` for child classification so an authorized symlink row does not become a target-kind oracle for an outside path.

Plugin lifecycle rollback now covers the VM dictionary/module topology as well as editor-owned runtime registrations. Failed plugin source evaluation, startup lifecycle failures, unload `deinit` failures, and reload rollback paths restore wordlists, module mappings, search order, current/next wordlist ids, module stack, and loaded-path evidence before restoring command/key/hook/timer registrations.

The validation runner gained an interruption boundary. While a pytest child is active, `tools/mxtest.py` catches normal termination signals, cleans the confirmed child process group, and leaves partial interrupted evidence for aggregate manifests. Test wrappers now default `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` to reduce pycache churn during ordinary cloudtainer validation.

Docs: `docs/718-dirfd-save-parent-swap.md`, `docs/719-fs-hostcalls-finalcontain-openread.md`, and `docs/720-mxtest-interrupt-plugin-dictionary-pycache.md`.

Rev769 note: scripted file operations now carry their `cap.fs-root` boundary down to the final read/write seam, and the validation runway now has a safer process-control boundary without letting doctor become a wasteful mini-full-suite. `write_file_bytes(...)` and `read_file_for_editor(...)` accept a `containment_root` and re-resolve the concrete target near the filesystem operation, so a late symlink swap after script preflight fails closed instead of reading or writing outside the script capability root. The editor save/save-as/diff/revert methods pass that root through the script-file-op helpers, and focused regressions cover atomic write, direct write, read, and scripted-save late-symlink escapes. In parallel, `tools/mxtest.py` now launches pytest children in their own process group/session when available, terminates confirmed child process groups on timeout, and avoids stale heartbeat noise at the completion boundary. `mxdoctor` is deliberately trimmed back to a bounded direct preflight with pytest capture disabled for its subprocess-control probes; full evidence remains explicit through `--full` or chunked mxtest. `mkrevzip` now reuses a current checked `MICROMAX-CONTEXT.json` snapshot before falling back to live context generation, which removes a wasteful repeated context rebuild/OOM risk in archive smoke tests. `docs/716-script-filecaps-final-containment.md` and `docs/717-mxtest-processgroup-fileisolated-doctor.md` record the audits/refactors.

Latest tiny landing (rev769): this keeps momentum on trust boundaries rather than registry churn. The important correction is that script file caps are enforced again at the final filesystem operation, not only at a higher-level command preflight, while mxtest timeout handling is safer and doctor stays small enough to be a real handoff gate instead of an accidental partial full-suite.

Rev768 note: validation evidence, script-visible disk state, plugin lifecycle execution, and hot command completion all gained trust boundaries. The rev768 mxtest lane adds parent-side heartbeat output for long quiet pytest children, explicit non-isolated `--chunk-timeout`, aggregate `running` checkpoint records before each non-empty chunk starts, and atomic JSON manifest writes so an interrupted checkpoint update does not corrupt the previous resume witness. The file-recovery inventory gap left by rev767 is now closed: `diskstate --all` uses the capability-gated script row helper when invoked through `ed.command`, structured `ed.disk-states` / `ed.disk-rows` hostcalls expose multi-buffer disk freshness rows, and scripted path-backed disk-state rows require `cap.fs-stat` plus honor `cap.fs-root` for relative paths. Plugin source and lifecycle hooks now restore stack-like VM execution state so plugin load/reload/unload cannot leave ordinary stack junk behind. Adding `diskstate` also exposed a prompt-completion regression; fuzzy fallback now keeps the best start-at-candidate band before offering hidden-substring command matches. `docs/714-mxtest-heartbeat-timeout-checkpoint.md` and `docs/715-diskstate-caprows-capboundary.md` record the audits/refactors.

Latest tiny landing (rev768): this keeps forward momentum on trust rather than registry churn. The test runway no longer has to be noisy just to look alive, checkpoint manifests are less fragile under interruption, and scripted disk-state inventory can no longer be used as an ambient filesystem stat oracle outside the explicit capability lane.

Rev767 note: scripted file operations now share a cap-root-aware file boundary, the low-level save writer now rechecks freshness immediately before commit, and `tools/mxtest.py` now keeps diagnostic verbosity from erasing chunk node-id selection. `src/micromax_editor/file_scriptops.py` anchors relative paths under `cap.fs-root` before save/diff/revert, the legacy `ed.save` hostcall now keeps its two-cell shape while using the transactional helper, and `src/micromax_editor/file_hostcalls.py` exposes structured `ed.disk-state`, `ed.diff`, `ed.revert`, `ed.save-info`, and `ed.save-as-info` recovery surfaces. This fixes a script-context capability bug where a relative buffer path could be validated against `cap.fs-root` but saved relative to cwd. In parallel, `src/micromax_editor/file_write.py` now carries a `FileFreshness` witness through to the atomic writer, rechecks just before `os.replace(...)`, cleans temp files on conflict, and treats permission-mode changes as stale-save evidence. The mxtest fix strips output-only flags such as `-vv` during collection while preserving them for execution, and records empty over-partitioned chunks as skipped passes instead of invoking pytest with no explicit node ids. `docs/712-script-filecaps-structured-recovery.md` and `docs/713-mxtest-verbosity-empty-selection-safety.md` record the audits/refactors.

Latest tiny landing (rev767): this continues the file-IO trust lane with actual script/plugin parity rather than another human-only command, narrows the remaining write race left by rev766, and hardens the validation runway itself. The important corrections are that script-owned saves now write the path that was authorized, ordinary saves refuse late external changes before replacing the file, and diagnostic mxtest flags can no longer turn an exact chunk into an accidental broad pytest run.

Rev766 note: save-conflict handling now has a user-facing recovery loop. `diff`/`diskdiff` inspect disk-vs-buffer changes, `revert` reloads clean stale buffers, `revert!` discards local edits with an undoable buffer-text step, and `save!` is the explicit overwrite path. The save guard now records a missing-file sentinel, checks small-file content hashes at the save boundary, refuses same-turn save-as create races, and keeps status disk warnings stat-cheap instead of hashing during every render. `docs/711-save-conflict-recovery-statcheap.md` records the audit/refactor.

Latest tiny landing (rev766): this closes the practical recovery gap left by rev765 without turning the statusline into an I/O hotspot. The editor now says what changed, lets the user inspect/reload/force intentionally, and keeps the expensive content digest on save/recovery boundaries rather than screen redraws.

Rev749 also hardens the chunked test runway with richer JSON chunk manifests and consistent pytest-arg forwarding.

Rev728 note: regex hostcall match maps now keep optional capture absence honest: unmatched groups and named captures are surfaced as the VM absent sentinel `0` instead of the literal text `"None"`, while captures that really matched an empty string stay `""`; `docs/669-regex-hostcall-unmatched-capture-hygiene.md` records why that small trust fix matters.

Latest tiny landing (rev728): this is a small regex-hostcall trust follow-up to the recent replacement-template runway. The regex hostcalls already returned compact match maps for `re.search` and `re.findall`, but the capture conversion layer still used `str(...)` for every capture. In Python, an optional capture that does not participate is `None`, so Micromax exposed it as the literal string `"None"`. That made true absence indistinguishable from text that actually matched those four characters, and it also broke the VM convention that absent/false values use `0`. Rev728 keeps the change deliberately narrow: match-map `groups` and `groupdict` values now convert unmatched captures to `0`, matched empty-string captures remain `""`, normal string captures remain unchanged, full-match `group` remains a string, and `re.sub` / `re.subn` behavior is untouched. Focused tests pin both `re-search` and `re-findall` so scripts and future host ports can rely on the distinction. The goal is simple: a regex match map should never turn absence into plausible user text.

Latest tiny landing (rev727): this is a small replacement-template parser follow-up to rev725's braced-token validation and rev726's canonical numeric references. Closed malformed braces already stayed literal, but unterminated `${...` forms still fell back to character-by-character parsing. That meant a typo such as `${$1` or `${x$1` could expand the nested `$1` even though the outer braced placeholder was malformed. Rev727 keeps the fix deliberately narrow: when the parser sees `${` without a matching `}`, it preserves the rest of the replacement template literally, with raw backslashes still protected from Python's replacement parser. Valid `${name}` / `${1}` references, malformed closed `${...}` literals, canonical numeric references, and invalid missing-group diagnostics remain unchanged. The goal is simple: once a braced replacement starts malformed, no hidden dollar token inside that malformed span should become active.

Latest tiny landing (rev726): this is a small replacement-template parser follow-up to rev725's braced-token validation. Rev725 made malformed braced templates literal, but canonical-looking numeric references still had one Python inheritance leak: `$01` and `${01}` were converted to `\g<01>`, and Python treats that as group 1. That meant the visible Micromax token could name one thing while the underlying replacement parser used another. Rev726 keeps the fix deliberately narrow: active numeric references must now use canonical decimal spelling (`$0`, `$1`, `$12`, `${0}`, `${1}`, `${12}`), while leading-zero numeric tokens such as `$01`, `$00`, and `${01}` are emitted as literal user text. Named references and valid missing-group diagnostics remain unchanged. The goal is simple: if a replacement token becomes active, the token the user sees should be the token the engine uses.

Latest tiny landing (rev725): this is a small replacement-template parser follow-up to rev724's sentinel-free rewrite. Rev724 made the converter single-pass, but braced references still accepted any non-empty `${...}` body and handed it to Python as `\g<...>`. That left one surprising delimiter leak: `${x>tail}` became `\g<x>tail>`, so a malformed Micromax placeholder could expand group `x` and leave `tail>` as literal text. Rev725 keeps the fix deliberately narrow: braced references now activate only when the body is a valid Micromax group token — ASCII digits or an ASCII identifier — while malformed closed braces are emitted as one literal segment with raw backslashes still protected from Python's replacement parser. Valid missing groups such as `${missing}` still fail through the existing invalid-replacement diagnostics; malformed placeholders such as `${x>tail}`, `${$1}`, and `${1abc}` now round-trip literally across editor replace, query-replace, and regex hostcalls. The goal is simple: braces should make replacement references clearer, not open a second delimiter language underneath Micromax's dollar dialect.

Latest tiny landing (rev718): this is a small trust follow-up to rev716/rev717's macro replay runway. Macro playback was already all-or-nothing for visible buffer text, cursor/selection state, active-buffer state, and command-history noise, but the rollback path still rebuilt `EditorBuffer` objects and left any undo entries recorded by the failed replay on the undo stack. Because undo/redo edits close over the original editor buffer object, that meant an aborted macro could look restored while older undo history silently pointed at a stale detached object, and a pre-existing redo lane could be cleared by automation that never completed. Rev718 keeps the fix local and inspectable: `UndoManager.snapshot()` / `restore(...)` capture undo/redo stack membership, `MacroReplaySnapshot` carries that stack snapshot, buffer rollback mutates the original `EditorBuffer` objects back into their saved state instead of replacing them, and focused tests prove both prior undo and prior redo still work after an aborted macro. The goal is simple: failed automation should not steal or strand the user's next recovery action.

The editor already had a grouped searchable `pluginpick`, a plain `plugin list` summary, explicit post-reload feedback, and a `plugin info NAME` path that began with the same inventory dialect, but filtered error inspection still lagged behind: `plugin errors NAME` could make an unknown plugin look the same as a healthy known plugin with no recorded errors, and it skipped the summary line that nearby plugin paths already used. Rev350 keeps the implementation deliberately small while making error inspection more honest: filtered `plugin errors NAME` now begins with the same summary entry shape used by `plugin list` / `plugin reload` / `plugin info`, known plugins say clearly when they currently have no recorded errors, truly unknown plugin names fail explicitly, focused tests pin down all three paths, and `docs/292-plugin-errors-feedback.md` records the intent. The goal is simple: plugin error inspection should tell you whether you queried the wrong plugin, a healthy plugin, or a broken plugin without making you infer it from the same `(none)` result.

Rev349 note: `plugin info NAME` starts with an explicit inventory summary now too — instead of opening with the older vague `plugin NAME: loaded|not loaded` header, plugin detail now begins with the same small inspectable dialect used by `plugin list` and `plugin reload` (`plugin info: name [loaded|error|available, ...]`), and truly unknown plugin names now fail plainly as `plugin info: no such plugin: NAME`; `docs/291-plugin-info-feedback.md` records why that small trust/flow follow-up matters.

Latest tiny landing (rev349): this is a small trust/flow follow-up to rev347/rev348's plugin-inventory honesty pass. Rev349 keeps the implementation deliberately small while making plugin inspection more honest: `plugin info NAME` now starts with the same summary entry shape used by `plugin list` / `plugin reload`, picker-driven info inherits that same first line automatically, unknown plugin names fail explicitly, focused tests pin down both the summary shape and the no-such-plugin path, and `docs/291-plugin-info-feedback.md` records the intent. The goal is simple: plugin detail should begin by telling the truth about plugin state, not by making users infer which kind of “not loaded” they are looking at.

Rev348 note: successful `plugin reload NAME` is explicit now too — instead of only saying `reloaded NAME`, plugin reload now reports the post-reload plugin state in the same small inspectable dialect as `plugin list` (`plugin reload: name [loaded|error|available, ...]`), so refreshing a plugin and inspecting plugin health no longer speak at different fidelity levels; `docs/290-plugin-reload-feedback.md` records why that small trust/flow follow-up matters.

Latest tiny landing (rev348): this is a small trust/flow follow-up to rev347's plugin-inventory honesty pass. Rev347 note: the plain `plugin list` inventory is explicit now too — `plugin list` no longer collapses plugin state to raw names plus trailing error chatter, but instead shows each plugin as a small inspectable entry with `loaded` / `available` / `error` state plus visible version/dependency hints; `docs/289-plugin-inventory-feedback.md` records why that small trust/flow follow-up matters.

Latest tiny landing (rev346): this is a small trust/flow follow-up to the recent orientation, recovery, and inventory-honesty work. (+N more)` suffix, and `docs/287-recent-inventory-feedback.md` records the intent. The goal is simple: recent-file inventory should be legible, not merely enumerable.

Rev343 note: clipboard actions now speak more honestly too — `Copy` / `Cut` report selection counts and copied chars, successful `Paste` reports the inserted char count plus the landed target, and empty internal-clipboard `Paste` now says `paste: clipboard empty` instead of failing silently; `docs/285-clipboard-feedback.md` records why that small trust/flow follow-up matters.

Latest tiny landing (rev343): this is a small trust/flow follow-up to the recent orientation, recovery, and macro-honesty work. Latest tiny landing (rev342): this is a small trust-first follow-up to the recent orientation and macro-honesty work. - Shared portability follow-up (rev247): the JSON portability corpus now also covers tiny boot-stdlib `1+`, `1-`, `0>`, and `0<>`, so future Python/Rust/WASM hosts can replay one more small arithmetic/predicate slice directly from JSON instead of inferring it from `core.mx`; see `docs/189-portability-oneplus-oneminus-zeropreds.md`.

- Shared visible docs/help cue model (rev227): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny `docs_cues_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.docs-cues`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible docs/help roles and markdown-ish cue spans without scraping curses output or re-parsing visible row fragments by hand; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that docs-help cue surface too, and the minimal curses TUI reuses the same shared snapshot instead of recomputing those row-local cues ad hoc.

- Shared docs/help code-delimiter metadata (rev319): `docs_cues_model(lines, cols)` / `ed.docs-cues` rows now also expose focused `single_backtick_code_entries` and `multi_backtick_code_entries` plus matching row-local counts and top-level `single_backtick_code_rows`, `multi_backtick_code_rows`, `single_backtick_code_count`, and `multi_backtick_code_count`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible docs/help single-vs-multi-backtick code spelling without re-filtering broader `code_entries`.

- Shared docs/help code-span metadata (rev236): `docs_cues_model(lines, cols)` / `ed.docs-cues` rows now also expose parsed `code_entries` (`kind`, full/body spans, delimiter length, body text) plus small top-level code counts, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible docs/help inline-code tokens without scraping raw backticks or rerunning the tiny shared matcher.

- Shared docs/help image-target slices (rev308): `docs_cues_model(lines, cols)` / `ed.docs-cues` rows now also expose focused `local_doc_image_entries`, `fragment_image_entries`, `external_image_entries`, and `footnote_image_entries` plus matching row-local counts and top-level `*_image_rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible docs/help image targets without re-filtering broader `image_entries`.
- Shared docs/help image target metadata (rev235): `docs_cues_model(lines, cols)` / `ed.docs-cues` rows now also expose parsed `image_entries` (`kind`, source spans, alt text, `target`, `target_kind`, `target_doc`, `target_fragment`) plus small top-level image counts, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect what visible docs/help image tokens point to without scraping raw markdown or rerunning the tiny shared matcher.
- Shared display-text screen-row model + scrollbar-thumb viewport-key fix (rev225): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny `display_rows_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.display-rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the final plain row text the reference curses TUI paints after `showchars` and horizontal overflow-marker overlays without scraping curses or replaying tiny overlay rules by hand; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that painted-text row surface too, the minimal curses TUI reuses the same shared viewport display text/overflow cells, and the shared gutter model now also reads the honest plain-view `top_line` viewport key when placing scrollbar thumbs.

- Shared visible `showchars` row model (rev224): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny viewport-local `showchars_rows_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.showchars-rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible `showchars` replacement text (`display_text`) and replacement spans without re-parsing the option or redoing fragment-local indent rules in renderer code; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that cue model too, and the minimal curses TUI reuses the same shared data instead of recomputing visible replacement spans ad hoc.

- Shared visible search-row model (rev223): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny viewport-local `search_rows_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.search-rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible edit-window search matches (`spans`) and the cursor-owned current visible match (`current_spans`) without scraping curses attributes or rebuilding row fragments by hand; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that cue model too, and the minimal curses TUI reuses the same shared data instead of recomputing visible `hlsearch` spans ad hoc.

- Shared plain screen-row model + nested-success visible-`r@` follow-up (rev222): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny flat `screen_rows_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.screen-rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the plain visible screen row-by-row without scraping curses or manually overlaying viewport rows, picker rows, and bottom chrome; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that flat row surface too, and the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that after a nested successful inner `catch`, the outer user-pushed return-stack item remains visible through `r@` as well as later removable through `r>`.

- Shared viewport-row model + nested-throw visible-`r@` follow-up (rev221): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny `viewport_rows_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.viewport-rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible edit-window rows already zipped together with line-number cells, scrollbar-thumb cells, and stable row/cursor positions without rejoining `edit_window_model(...)` and `gutter_model(...)` by hand; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that row-ordered surface too, and the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that after an inner `throw` is caught and control returns to the outer protected region, an outer user-pushed return-stack item remains visible through `r@` as well as later removable via `r>`.

# Worklist (rev313)
This is the **living, prioritized worklist** for Micromax + the micro-esque editor core.

- Shared gutter model + nested-throw visible-`rdepth` follow-up (rev220): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny `gutter_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.gutter-model`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect visible line-number cells and scrollbar-thumb rows without re-deriving the last bit of curses gutter logic from `tui.py`; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that gutter snapshot too, and the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that a nested inner `throw` still leaves only outer user-pushed items visible to `rdepth` once control returns to the outer protected region.

- Shared prompt-panel model + nested-success `catch`/visible-`rdepth` follow-up (rev219): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny `prompt_panel_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.prompt-panel`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the visible picker suggestion pane — reserved geometry plus positioned rendered entries — without recombining `screen_layout_model(...)` and `prompt_display_model(...)` by hand; the composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` now includes that panel too, and the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that nested successful `catch` frames stay hidden from visible `rdepth`, so only outer user-pushed return-stack items count.

- Shared visible-screen model + nested-throw `catch`/outer-`rstack` order follow-up (rev218): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny composed `screen_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.screen-model`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the whole visible reference screen — layout, edit-window rows, positioned bottom chrome, and active cursor placement — without stitching several smaller hostcalls together by hand; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that a nested inner `throw` still preserves the order of outer user-pushed return-stack items.

- Shared edit-window model + nested-success `catch`/outer-`rstack` order follow-up (rev217): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny reference `edit_window_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.edit-window`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the visible edit-window rows and primary cursor screen position without reverse-engineering curses viewport math; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that a successful *nested* `catch` still preserves the order of outer user-pushed return-stack items.

- Shared screen-layout model + successful-`catch`/outer-`rstack` order follow-up (rev216): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny reference `screen_layout_model(lines, cols)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.screen-layout`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect viewport size, gutter widths, picker suggestion height, and bottom-row y-positions without reverse-engineering curses math; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that a successful `catch` preserves the order of outer user-pushed return-stack items, not just throw-time unwinds.

- Shared interaction-row model + outer-`rstack` order portability follow-up (rev215): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny width-aware `interaction_model(width)` snapshot plus hostcall `ed.interaction-model`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the visible prompt/capture row (`raw_line`, visible `text`, truncation, shared prompt/capture metadata) without scraping curses output; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that `catch`/`throw` preserve the order of outer user-pushed return-stack items, not just their depth or one surviving value.

- Shared statusline-layout model + nested-`catch` portability follow-up (rev213): the shared editor core now publishes one tiny `statusline_model(width)` snapshot (`left_raw`, `right_raw`, visible `left` / `right`, padding width, truncation flags, final `text`) through `ed.statusline-model`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect statusline layout without re-parsing the rendered row; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that an inner `throw` inside a successful outer `catch` still preserves outer user-owned return-stack items.

- Shared bottom-row chrome model + outer-`rstack` recovery-contract follow-up (rev212): the shared editor core now publishes the exact visible bottom chrome stack as ordered row maps (`kind`, `slot`, `text`) through `bottom_rows_model(width)` / `ed.bottom-rows`, so future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect keymenu / interaction-or-infobar / statusline rows without reverse-engineering curses output; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that `throw` inside `catch` preserves outer user-pushed return-stack items.

- Shared bottom-row interaction snapshot + successful-`catch`/user-`rdepth` portability follow-up (rev211): the shared editor status model now also publishes one tiny unified prompt/capture snapshot (`interaction_kind`, `interaction_summary`, `interaction_detail`, `interaction_position`, `interaction_line`) so future UIs/statuslines/scripts/LLMs can inspect the active bottom-row interaction without rebuilding TUI text from scattered prompt/capture fields; the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that successful `catch` counts only user-pushed return-stack items in visible `rdepth`.

- Tiny capture-prompt/keymenu honesty pass + `catch` return-stack portability follow-up (rev209): existing capture keymodes like `qreplace` and `openurl` now get a real one-line bottom-row prompt plus mode-aware keymenu hints in the minimal curses TUI, even when `infobar=false`, and the JSON portability corpus now also pins down that `catch` restores return-stack depth on `throw` instead of leaking values left on `rstack`.

- Tiny `constantshow` infobar cue + portability corpus hygiene follow-up (rev208): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny nano-esque `constantshow` option so the idle infobar can keep a right-aligned line/column/percentage summary next to the last message without promoting more bottom-bar structure into the headless editor model, and the portability corpus now makes the older `return-stack-roundtrip` case honest by actually round-tripping with `r>` while the newer peek/depth case keeps `rdrop` coverage focused.

- Tiny `showchars` TUI pass + missing-map-predicate portability follow-up (rev203): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny micro-esque `showchars` option so ordinary and help/docs buffers can display visible one-cell replacements for spaces/tabs (with `ispace` / `itab` overrides in the leading indent run) without mutating buffer text; later rev224 lifts that same visible cue into a shared editor-side row model. The portability corpus now also covers `m?` returning `0` for a missing key.
- Shared `fastdirty` modified-state policy + portability follow-up (rev202): the shared editor core now treats `fastdirty` as a real ordinary option, so buffers can either keep the cheap “edited once means dirty” rule or accurately clear `dirty` again when the text returns to the last clean baseline; command-bar / Micromax option paths resync existing buffers immediately, and the portability corpus now also covers merging into an empty destination map adopting the source pairs.
- Shared `autosave` timed save path + portability follow-up (rev201): the shared editor core now treats `autosave` as a real ordinary option, so dirty path-backed buffers can save through the same headless save path after N seconds, `quit` can reuse that same autosave path for eligible dirty buffers, and the portability corpus now also covers merging an empty source map preserving destination pairs.
- Shared `basename` display-path option + portability follow-up (rev200): the shared editor core now treats the visible filename as real status/display state instead of a hardcoded basename, so `status_model()` carries both raw `file_name` and effective `display_name`, `$(filename)` / `showstatus` honor a tiny `basename` option, and the portability corpus now also covers `m-items` returning `[]` for an empty map.
- Real `encoding` open/save behavior + portability follow-up (rev199): the shared editor core now treats text encoding as a real per-buffer option instead of a hardcoded `utf-8` status token, so `open_file(...)` decodes with the configured encoding, `save()` writes with the effective buffer encoding, and status/config surfaces report the same user-facing value; the portability corpus now also covers `m-keys` returning `[]` for an empty map.
- Micro-esque `savehistory` option alias + portability follow-up (rev197): the editor's existing prompt-history persistence can now be enabled through `savehistory`, backed by a tiny alias-aware option registry so reads/writes/toggles and command-bar completion all resolve to the same canonical `history.persist` state; the portability corpus now also covers `m@` returning `0` for a missing key.
- Real `fileformat` open/save behavior + portability follow-up (rev196): the shared editor core now treats line endings as a real per-buffer option instead of a status-model placeholder, so `open_file(...)` best-effort detects CRLF files as `dos`, normalizes buffer text internally to `\n`, and `save()` writes LF or CRLF from the effective option value; the portability corpus now also pins down that *missing* `host.feature?` probes stay lookup-only with respect to `dict-version`.
- Leading-indent cursor stepping + portability follow-up (rev194): the shared editor core now honors a tiny `tabmovement` option so `CursorLeft` / `CursorRight` and `SelectLeft` / `SelectRight` can treat leading runs of `tabsize` spaces like one tab stop when `tabstospaces` is enabled, mirroring micro's conservative indent-only rule without promoting a richer visual-column subsystem; the portability corpus now also covers `host.api-version` staying lookup-only with respect to `dict-version`.
- Protected-buffer option + portability follow-up (rev193): the editor's existing protected-buffer path is now available through a tiny ordinary `readonly` option, so `setlocal readonly true` can block edits and saves in ordinary buffers while reusing the same shared behavior as help/docs buffers; the portability corpus now also covers `host.features` staying lookup-only with respect to `dict-version`.
- Whitespace-only autoindent cleanup + shared open-target parsing (rev192): the shared editor core now honors a tiny `keepautoindent` option so pressing Enter again on a whitespace-only autoindented line can either clear or keep that previous indent through the same headless `InsertNewline` path, and the rebuilt shared `parsecursor` path again understands `file:line[:col]` across interactive, capability-gated/scripted, persistence-aware, and path-palette opens.

- Smart multiline paste indentation + host-inventory portability follow-up (rev190): the shared editor core now honors a tiny `smartpaste` option so multi-line `Paste` can reuse the current line's existing whitespace prefix for otherwise-unindented blocks, keeping the behavior headless-first across ordinary, multi-cursor, and external/internal clipboard pastes without inventing a formatter; the portability corpus now also covers seeded `host.features` inventory deduplicating repeated feature names while staying sorted.

- Shared page-overlap paging + dictionary-version portability follow-up (rev185): the editor core now honors a tiny `pageoverlap` option so `PageUp` / `PageDown` keep a few rows from the previous view visible in both ordinary and softwrapped views, with overlap measured in visual rows under `softwrap`; the portability corpus now also covers `dict-version` staying stable across lookup-only `get-current`.

- Overflow-marker cue + dictionary-version portability follow-up (rev183): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny `overflowmarkers` option by drawing bold+dim `<` / `>` cues at the visible edges of horizontally clipped rows in ordinary buffers and docs/help buffers while intentionally suppressing them under `softwrap`, and the portability corpus now also covers `dict-version` staying stable across lookup-only `find`.

- Trailing-whitespace cue + search-order portability follow-up (rev177): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny `hltrailingws` option by reverse-dimming visible trailing spaces/tabs in ordinary buffers, docs/help buffers, and softwrapped final fragments without promoting whitespace-warning spans into the headless editor model, and the portability corpus now also covers `previous` dropping the top search-order entry.
- Tiny `cursorline` TUI pass + namespace portability follow-up (rev176): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny `cursorline` option by underlining the current visible buffer row (including the active wrapped screen row under `softwrap`) without promoting row-highlighting into the headless editor model, and the portability corpus now also covers `definitions` following the top of the current search order.
- Shared scrollmargin viewport pass + tiny portability follow-up (rev175): the editor core now honors a tiny `scrollmargin` option inside the shared viewport contract, keeping vertical context rows around the primary cursor in both ordinary and softwrapped views, and the portability corpus now also covers `compiled?` returning `0` for a primitive XT.

- Scrollbar thumb-character follow-up + search-order portability case (rev181): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny `scrollbarchar` option so the existing right-edge scrollbar cue can render a user-chosen one-cell glyph (default `|`, empty falls back to `|`, longer strings use their first character) without promoting new scrollbar state into the headless editor model, and the portability corpus now also covers search-order conflict precedence so future hosts can validate that the first search-order entry wins on duplicate names from JSON alone.

- Scrollbar cue + host-feature portability follow-up (rev180): the minimal curses TUI now honors a tiny `scrollbar` option by reserving one right-edge column for a proportional thumb in ordinary and softwrapped views without promoting scrollbar state into the headless editor model, and the portability corpus can now seed per-case `host_features` so future hosts can validate positive host-boundary probes directly from JSON.

- Statusline search-count cue + tiny portability follow-up (rev174): the reference statusline formatter now understands `$(searchpos)` / `$(searchcount)` / `$(search)` as a compact ` [i/n]` token built from the shared search-position model, the default right-side status format now includes that token so ordinary TUI/statusline rendering exposes active search position outside the transient find prompt too, and the portability corpus now also covers missing-word `find` returning `0`.

- Search-position/statusline win (rev173): the editor now exposes one shared whole-buffer search-position model through `status_model()` / `ed.status` (`search_query`, `search_literal`, `search_case_sensitive`, `search_match_index`, `search_match_count`, `search_summary`), the minimal TUI now shows the same compact `[i/n]` summary right in the find prompt, and future statuslines/UIs/LLMs can reuse that same tiny contract instead of re-counting matches themselves.

- Portability host-boundary follow-up (rev173): `portability/kernel_cases.json` now also covers bare-VM `host.feature?` for a missing feature, giving future Rust/WASM hosts one more tiny host-introspection contract point alongside `host.api-version` and bare-VM `host.features`.

- Portability exact-slice + missing-kernel-contract win (rev170): the tiny JSON portability suite now covers a few more missing but very contract-like kernel behaviors — `0=`, successful `catch`, list clone/pop isolation, `m-keys`, `m-items`, session-local query/removal, and `set-current` round-tripping — and `mxportable` / `portability_suite.py` now support exact case-name filtering so future hosts and future LLMs can ask for precise slices without fuzzy substring matching.

- Portability inventory + kernel-coverage win (rev169): the tiny JSON portability suite now covers a few more portable kernel behaviors Micromax already relies on — `when`, `to-int`, `to-str`, `m-del`, `m-merge` — and `mxportable` can now emit a lighter-weight inventory of matching categories/tags/case names so future hosts and future LLMs can inspect the contract without pulling full case bodies.

- Portability inspectability win (rev168): the tiny JSON portability suite now covers a few more missing portable kernel behaviors — `while`, `constant`, `variable`, locals shadowing — carries broader tags across the corpus, and `mxportable` can now emit machine-readable JSON summaries/results so future hosts and future LLMs can inspect slices without scraping human text.

- Portability contract/tooling win (rev167): the tiny JSON portability suite now covers more missing portable kernel/boot-stdlib ground — return-stack basics, `execute`, wordlist/search-order lookup, `in`, `2dip`, `recover`, and `finally` — and the shared runner/CLI now validate case shape plus support category/tag/name filtering for targeted cross-host runs.

- Portability-corpus coverage win (rev166): the tiny JSON portability suite now covers more of the portable language surface Micromax actually uses in anger — quotation combinators (`dip`, `2keep`, `tri`), recovery/cleanup helpers (`try?`, `try`, `ensure`), and budget exhaustion under `catch` — so future Rust/WASM hosts can validate more than arithmetic + one `bi` example against the Python oracle.

- Docs/help multi-line footnote-definition render-parity win (rev165): tiny indented continuation lines under markdown footnote definitions now also render dim in the live docs/help TUI, while visible `[^id]:` starter markers keep their existing dim+bold treatment, all through the same shared definition-line roles instead of another parser path.

- Docs/help escaped-markdown source-view cue win (rev164): visible backslash-escaped markdown punctuation pairs like `\[` / `\!` / `\<` / `\*` / `\_` / `\~` / ``\``` now also read a little more like deliberate source in the live docs/help TUI: the visible two-character escape pair renders dim while the escaped form still stays literal and non-navigable, all through one tiny helper layered on the existing shared backslash-escape policy instead of another parser path.

- Docs/help inline-code delimiter cue win (rev163): visible markdown inline-code backtick delimiters now also read a little more like deliberate source in the live docs/help TUI: opening/closing backtick runs render bold+dim while the already-dim code body styling stays intact, all through one tiny shared helper layered on the existing equal-length backtick scan instead of another parser path.
- Docs/help live-link source-scaffolding cue win (rev161): visible supported ordinary markdown links like `[Vision](00-vision.md)` / `[Vision ref][visionref]` / `[Vision shortcut]` now also read a little more like deliberate source in the live docs/help TUI: the non-label scaffolding (`[` / `](` + destination or reference tail / `]`) renders dim while the live label stays underlined, all through one tiny helper layered on the existing shared link matcher instead of another parser path.
- Docs/help inline raw-HTML source-view cue win (rev160): visible inline raw HTML tags like `<kbd>` / `</kbd>` / `<a name=...>` now also read a little more like deliberately inert source in the live docs/help TUI: whole tag tokens render dim through one tiny helper layered on the existing shared inline raw-HTML span helper, while supported autolinks keep their separate bold whole-token cue.

- Docs/help inline-emphasis delimiter cue win (rev162): visible markdown emphasis delimiters like `**` / `*` / `_` / `~~` now also read a little more like deliberate source in the live docs/help TUI: delimiter tokens render dim while the existing strong/emphasis/strike body styling stays intact, all through one tiny helper layered on the existing inline-emphasis regex helpers instead of another parser path.

It is intentionally practical:
- describes *what users actually feel* (keybindings, editing loops)
- breaks work into pieces small enough to land with tests
- records **status** and **pointers** (files/tests) so future humans/LLMs can pick up the thread quickly

- Docs/help image-token inert-source cue win (rev159): visible supported markdown image forms like `![alt](dest)` / `![alt][id]` now also read a little more like deliberately inert source in the live docs/help TUI: the whole token renders dim, all through one tiny helper layered on the existing shared markdown destination/reference helpers instead of another parser path.
- Docs/help autolink-token source-view cue win (rev158): visible supported angle-bracket autolinks like `<https://...>` now also read a little more like autolink tokens in the live docs/help TUI: the whole token renders bold while the inner URL still keeps the ordinary docs-link underline, all through one tiny helper layered on the existing shared autolink matcher instead of another parser path.
- Docs/help footnote-reference source-view cue win (rev157): visible markdown footnote references like `[^note]` now also read a little more like footnote tokens in the live docs/help TUI: the whole token renders bold while the inner `^note` still keeps the ordinary docs-link underline, all through one tiny helper layered on the existing shared footnote matcher instead of another parser path.
- Docs/help nested-list scanability win (rev155): the live docs/help TUI now opts into deeper indentation for nested list/task markers only after the shared indented-code check has already ruled out real top-level code blocks, so nested worklist bullets finally read like lists in source view without reclassifying literal code examples.
- Docs/help definition render-parity win (rev154): reference-style definition starter lines now render dim with a bold `[id]:` token, tiny wrapped reference-definition continuation lines render dim too, and footnote-definition starter lines get the same dim+bold treatment in the live docs/help TUI — all through one shared definition-line helper reused from the docs-browser side instead of another render-only parser.
- Docs/help raw-HTML render-parity win (rev153): raw HTML comments and raw HTML block lines were already inert for docs navigation, definition parsing, and link underlining; the live docs/help TUI now dims those existing comment spans / block lines too, so commented-out notes and embedded HTML examples read less like ordinary prose without adding another parser path.
- Docs/help indented-code parity win (rev152): blank-separated top-level indented code-ish lines now stay inert for `helpfollow`, `helplinkpick`, and TUI docs-link underlining too, while the live docs/help TUI dims those lines via one tiny shared helper so source-view examples read like code instead of accidentally clickable prose.
- Docs/help fenced-code render-scanability win (rev151): docs/help rendering now reuses one tiny shared fenced-code line-role helper, so fenced markdown examples finally look visibly code-ish in the live TUI too: opening/closing fence lines render bold+dim while fenced body lines render dim, staying aligned with the same fence precedence already trusted for docs-link actions and definition parsing.
- Docs/help setext-heading render-parity win (rev150): docs/help rendering now reuses the same tiny heading scan already trusted for titles / outline rows / fragment jumps / heading breadcrumbs, so setext heading title lines finally render like headings in the live TUI too while their underline line gets a dim heading-ish treatment.
- Docs/help list-marker scanability win (rev149): the minimal TUI now gives ordinary markdown list markers (`-` / `+` / `*`, `1.` / `1)`) a little scanability polish via one shared helper, so list-heavy docs and worklists read a bit more like real markdown while task-list checkbox styling continues to layer on top.
- Prompt-display truncation/readability win (rev148): shared visible picker rows now preserve trailing path/location detail when widths get tight instead of naively chopping the whole row at the right edge; tiny headers/more markers also ellipsize a bit more cleanly, keeping the shared `prompt_display_model` slightly closer to a real picker surface.
- Prompt-window/shared-scroll-model win (rev146): the minimal TUI's picker windowing now lives in one shared editor helper (`prompt_window_model`) instead of only inside `tui.py`, and future UIs/scripts/LLMs can inspect the same sticky-header / more-marker / hidden-count structure headlessly through `ed.prompt-window`.
- Docs-focused picker browse-budget parity (rev144, rev268): `helplinkpick` and `helpnavpick` now flatten their grouped help sections through the same shared browse-window policy as the other section-aware pickers, so empty-query docs browsing keeps `Docs` / `Files` / `External` and honest outline-breadcrumb heading groups (`Top`, document title, deeper parent paths) visible instead of hiding later sections behind a large first bucket.
- Helpnav heading-breadcrumb parity (rev268): the combined docs navigator now starts from `help_outline_section_rows(...)` for its heading half, so `helpnavpick`, TUI headers/sticky headers, `Alt-Up` / `Alt-Down`, and prompt preview/status surfaces can all agree on outline-breadcrumb heading labels instead of one generic `Headings` region.
- Recent-dir picker parity (rev267): the live editor now also has `recentdirpick`, which flattens the same directory-grouped recent rows already exposed through `ed.recent-dir-section-rows`, so TUI headers/sticky headers, `Alt-Up` / `Alt-Down`, and prompt preview/status surfaces can all agree on parent-directory labels without overloading project-root `recentpick`.
- Help-outline grouped-section parity (rev266): `helpoutlinepick` now flattens grouped parent-heading sections on empty-query browse, future UIs/scripts can read that same shape through `ed.help-outline-section-rows`, and prompt preview/status surfaces now reuse those same visible breadcrumb labels instead of one generic `Headings` bucket.
- Command-palette/live-grouping win (rev142): `commandpick` now flattens the same grouped palette sections already exposed through `ed.command-palette-section-rows`, so the live prompt, TUI section headers, `Alt-Up` / `Alt-Down`, and prompt preview/status surfaces all agree on visible labels like `Recent Files`, `Recent`, `Commands`, and `Actions`. Empty-query browse windows also now budget rows across visible sections so commands do not consume the whole first page before actions appear.
- Topic-picker/live-grouping win (rev141): `topicpick` now flattens the same grouped topic families already exposed through `ed.topic-section-rows` / `ed.apropos-section-rows`, so the live prompt, TUI section headers, `Alt-Up` / `Alt-Down`, and prompt preview/status surfaces all agree on `Commands` / `Actions` / `Words`. Empty-query `topicpick` also now budgets the first row window across visible sections so Actions/Words remain browseable instead of disappearing behind the command list cap.
- Recent picker/readability win (rev140): picker-style prompts now expose one shared current-item position model (`prompt_current_position`, status-model `prompt_*` ordinal fields, and `ed.prompt-current-position`), and the minimal TUI prompt line now shows that same compact `index/count • section i/n` summary next to the existing preview so long grouped pickers answer “where am I?” consistently across headless status/debug surfaces and the live UI.
- Previous recent picker/readability win (rev139): `recentpick` now flattens the same shared project-root grouping already exposed through `ed.recent-section-rows`, so the live prompt, TUI headers/sticky headers, `Alt-Up` / `Alt-Down`, and prompt preview/status surfaces all agree on one recent-files section model. Recent rows also rewrite their visible detail to project-name + project-relative path for faster scanning.

- Previous picker/readability win (rev138): `helppick` now groups docs rows by numbered doc families (`00–09 Project`, `10–19 Research`, `20–29 Language + VM`, etc.) and exposes that same shape through `ed.doc-section-rows`, while `prompt_current_section` / preview / status now reuse those same visible labels and docs previews now lead with the human doc title instead of the slug topic.

- Previous picker/readability win (rev137): `bindingpick` now groups rows by winning mode and exposes that same shape through `ed.binding-section-rows`, while `prompt_current_section` / preview / status now reuse those same visible labels (`Prompt`, active mode names like `nav`, `Global`) instead of the generic `Binding` bucket.

- Recent docs-browser win (rev134): docs/help navigation now keeps empty and whitespace-only markdown labels literal prose across inline/reference/shortcut links, so malformed cases like `[](doc.md)` and `[ ](doc.md)` no longer leak into `helpfollow`, `helplinkpick`, or TUI underlining.

- Previous docs-browser win (rev133): docs/help navigation now refuses outer markdown links whose label text contains a valid inner link, keeping the outer prose literal while still exposing the valid inner link through `helpfollow`, `helplinkpick`, and TUI underlining.

- Previous docs-browser win (rev132): docs/help navigation now also covers tiny wrapped angle-bracket destinations (`<path with spaces.md>`) in inline links and reference definitions, while the same shared destination helper now rejects malformed title starts that appear immediately after a closing `>` with no separating whitespace.
- Previous docs-browser win (rev131): docs/help inline links now also support tiny wrapped forms where the destination can sit on the next line and the title/closing `)` can wrap once as well, with one shared continuation helper keeping `helpfollow`, `helplinkpick`, and TUI docs-link underlining aligned around fenced/raw-HTML/comment-hidden lines.

- Previous docs-browser win (rev130): docs/help block starters now keep a shared spaces-only 0–3 indent guard, so four-space and tab-indented reference definitions / footnote definitions / ATX heading lookalikes stay code-ish prose instead of leaking into navigation, pickers, or outline rows.
- TUI search-highlight win (rev172): the minimal curses TUI now honors the existing `hlsearch` option, reverse-highlighting visible matches for the current search pattern and bolding the visible current match under the primary cursor, while keeping the feature renderer-local instead of promoting search-highlight spans into headless editor state.
- Portability corpus follow-up (rev172): `portability/kernel_cases.json` now also covers typed string ordering via `s<` plus the bare-kernel `host.features` default shape, giving future Rust/WASM hosts one more tiny typed-string contract point and one more host-boundary inventory check.

- Previous docs-browser win (rev127): common raw HTML blocks now stay inert through one shared block helper, so helpfollow/helplinkpick/outline scans/reference+footnote defs/TUI underlining agree on `<div>`/`<pre>`-style precedence.

## Status legend

- ✅ **DONE** — implemented + covered by tests
- 🧪 **VERIFY** — exists, but needs an end-to-end test or audit
- 🧱 **TODO** — not implemented yet
- 💤 **DEFER** — postponed intentionally (needs UI layer or bigger design)
- 💡 **IDEA** — plausible, but not currently committed

## A gentle argument against “Tier 6 is late”

The list below is excellent, but there’s one strategic twist:

> **A minimal TUI loop should *not* wait until Tier 6.**

Even a 200-line curses wrapper (input → `ed.press-key` → render) is a multiplier:
- it will expose subtle movement/editing bugs immediately
- it makes it easier to judge what headless APIs are missing
- it prevents the “1000 perfect headless features, 0 usability” trap

So: we keep most rendering sophistication late, but we try to get a *tactile* loop early.

## Tier 0 — Editor must be functional

1. **Verify `save` writes to disk.** ✅ DONE (rev61): `Editor.save()` writes `Buffer.path` to disk and clears `dirty`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_tier0_basics.py::test_open_and_save_round_trip`.

2. **Verify `open` loads a file.** ✅ DONE (rev61): `Editor.open_file()` reads UTF-8 or starts empty if missing. Same test as above.

3. **`quit` warns on unsaved changes.** ✅ DONE (rev61): `quit` arms once when any buffer is dirty, then quits on second attempt; `quit -f` / `quit!` force. Code: `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_tier0_basics.py::test_quit_warns_on_dirty_buffers_and_can_force`.

3b. **Close buffer (`close`, `close!`).** ✅ DONE (rev74): close current or named buffer with a dirty-buffer double-tap guard (mirrors `quit`). Code: `src/micromax_editor/editor.py::close_buffer`, `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_buffer_lifecycle_recent.py`.

3c. **Bulk buffer close + previous buffer MRU.** ✅ DONE (rev75): `closeall`, `only`, and `prevbuf`, plus MRU-based active-buffer selection after close. Code: `src/micromax_editor/editor.py` (MRU), `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_buffer_mru_and_closeall.py`.

3d. **Explicit undo/redo recovery feedback.** ✅ DONE (rev342): key-driven `Undo` / `Redo` now report the traversed edit description plus touched target, command-bar `undo` / `redo` now exist as tiny mirrors, and empty-stack retries fail explicitly instead of mutating or failing silently. Code: `src/micromax_editor/editor.py`, `src/micromax_editor/undo.py`, `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Docs: `docs/32-undo-and-history.md`, `docs/55-editor-command-bar.md`, `docs/284-undo-redo-feedback.md`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_core.py`.

3e. **Explicit clipboard loop feedback.** ✅ DONE (rev343): `Copy` / `Cut` now report selection counts and copied-char totals, successful `Paste` now reports cursor count, inserted chars, and the landed target, and empty internal-clipboard `Paste` now says `paste: clipboard empty` instead of failing silently. Code: `src/micromax_editor/editor.py`. Docs: `docs/50-editor-behaviors.md`, `docs/87-editor-config.md`, `docs/285-clipboard-feedback.md`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_core.py`, `tests/test_editor_clipboard_external_import.py`.
3f. **Explicit plain buffer inventory.** ✅ DONE (rev344, rev365 follow-up): `buffers` no longer collapses open state to raw names, but instead shows the active marker, visible `dirty` / `readonly` flags, and current cursor target for each open buffer; rev365 adds a tiny count-aware `buffers: N buffer(s)` prefix so the same inventory stays glanceable in both zero and non-zero cases. Code: `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Docs: `docs/50-editor-behaviors.md`, `docs/55-editor-command-bar.md`, `docs/286-buffer-inventory-feedback.md`, `docs/307-buffer-mark-counts.md`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_pickers_buffers_marks.py`, `tests/test_editor_statusline.py`, `tests/test_editor_main_cli.py`.
3g. **Explicit plain recent-file inventory.** ✅ DONE (rev345): `recent` no longer collapses MRU state to raw numbered paths, but instead shows active/open state, visible `dirty` / `readonly` flags, and current cursor targets for still-open recent files so ordinary file history becomes legible without entering `recentpick`. Code: `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Docs: `docs/50-editor-behaviors.md`, `docs/55-editor-command-bar.md`, `docs/287-recent-inventory-feedback.md`. Tests: `tests/test_editor_buffer_lifecycle_recent.py`.
3h. **Explicit plain plugin inventory.** ✅ DONE (rev347): `plugin list` no longer collapses plugin state to raw names plus trailing error chatter, but instead shows each plugin with visible `loaded` / `available` / `error` state plus version/dependency hints so ordinary plugin health becomes legible without entering `pluginpick` or `plugin info/errors`. 3j. **Explicit plugin-info summary feedback.** ✅ DONE (rev349): `plugin info NAME` no longer opens with the older `plugin NAME: loaded|not loaded` header, but instead begins with the same state/version/dependency summary entry shape used by `plugin list` / `plugin reload`, and truly unknown plugin names now fail plainly as `plugin info: no such plugin: NAME` so plugin detail no longer makes users infer which kind of missing state they are looking at. Code: `src/micromax_editor/command_dispatcher.py`. Docs: `docs/50-editor-behaviors.md`, `docs/95-plugin-json.md`, `docs/293-plugin-error-inventory-feedback.md`.
